ORDER 

New Delhi, the 24th March, 1976 

S.O. 1347. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri Madanbhai Udesing Chaiidhary, At Dungri (Pana- 
falia), Post Vanskui, Taluka Mahuva, District Surat (Gujarat), 
a contesting candidate for general election to the Gujarat 
Legislative Assembly from 165-Mahiiva Constituency, held in 
June, 1975 bos failed to lod^e an account of his election ex- 
penses within the time and in the manner required by the 
Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made 
thereunder ; 

And whereas, after considering the representation made by 
the said candidate, the Election Commission is satisfied that 
he has no good reason or justification for the failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of Section lOA of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Madanbhai Udesing Chaudhary to be disqualified for 
being chosen as, and for being, a member of either House 
of Parliament or of the Legislative Assembly or LcfitsHtRc 
Council of a State for a period of three years from the date 
of this order. 

[No, GJ-LA/165/75(36)] 
By Order, 

A. N. SEN, Secy.

S.O. 1348. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shii Ramabhai Shanabhai Solanki, Halupiiia, Junu 


Rasta, Anand, District Kaira (Gujarat), a contesting candi- 
date for general election to the Gujarat Legislative Assembly 
from 127-Umrclh Constituency held in June, 1975 has failed 
to lodge an account of his election expenses in the manner rc- 
auired by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, and 
the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas, after considering the representation made 
by the said candidate, the Election Commission is satisfied 
that ho has no good reason or justification for the failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of Section lOA of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Ramabhai Shanabhai Solanki to be disqualified for being 
cho^n as, and for being, a member of cither House of 
Parliament or of the Legislative A.ssembly or Legislalivc 
Council of a State for a period of three years from the date 
of this order.

S.O. 1333.— In exercise of the powers conferred by clause 
(b) of sub-section (1) of section 9 of the Indian Stamps Act,

of fifty-five lakhs of rupees issued by the said Corporation.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM 


(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 23rd, March, 1976 

S. O. 1380— Whereas by a notification of the Govt, of India 
in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Department of 
petroleum SO.No. 1631 dated 14-4-75 under 8ub-section(0 of 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right 
of User In Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Govern- 
ment declared its intention to acquit e the Right to User in the 
lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipelines ; 

And Whereas the Competent Authority has under sub- 
section (I) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to the 
Government ; 

And Further Whereas the Central Government has 
after considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the scheduled appended to this 
notification ; 

Now Therefore in exercise of the power conferred by 
sub-section(l) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central <7ovcni 
ment hereby declares that the right of aser in the said lands 
specified in the schedule appended to this notification hereby 
acquired for laying the pipelines ; 

And Further in exercise of the power conferred by sub- 
section (4) of that Section, the Central Government directs that 
the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the 
Central Government vest on this date of the publication of this 
declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free from all 
encumbrances.

S.O. 1382.— "Whereas by a notification of the 
Govt, of India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals 
(Department of Petroleum) S.O.No. 5139 Dated 6-12-75 under 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisi- 
tion of Right of User in land) Act, 1 962 (50 of 1962), the Central 
Government declared its intention to acquire the Right of User 
in the lands specified in the schedule appended to that notifi- 
cation for the put pose of laying pipelines; 

And whereas the Competent Authotity has under sub 
section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to the 
Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has 
after considering the said report, decided to acquire the right 
of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now therefore in exercise of the Power conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central Go- 
vernment hereby declares that the right of user in the said lands 
specified in the schedule appended to this notification hereby 
acquired for laying the pipelines; 

And further in exercise of the power conferred by sub- 
section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs that 
the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the 
Central Government vest on the date of the publication of this 
declaration in the Indian Oil Corporation Limited free front all 
encumberances.

S.O. 1400.— In exercise of the powers conferred by 
clauses (f) and (g) of sub-section (I) of section 47 of the 
Indian Railways Act. 1890 (9 of 1890), the Central Gov- 
ernment hereby makes the following rules further to amend 
the Railways (Warehousing and 'Affiarfage) Rules, 1938, 
published with the notification of the Government of India 
ill the Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) No. TCIII/ 
3036 /58-notification, dated the 28lh August, 1958, 
namely : — 

1. (1) Thesse rules may be called the Railways (Ware- 
hoasing and Wharfage) (Second .Amendment) Rules, 1976, 

(2) Thev shall come into force on the first day of 
May, 1976. 

2. In lule 6 of the Railways (Warehousing and Wharfage 
Rules, 1958. in the table appearing under the heading 
“Demurrage on goods wagons”, ag.ainst each of the items 
(i) and (ii) under column I, under column 3, for the figures 
and words "60 paise for all days for vegetable oil tank 
wagons", the figures, words and Ictteis “60 paise for ihe 
fast day. Re 1.00 foi the second day, and Rs. 2.00 for the 
third day and thereafter for vegetable oil lank wagons” 
.shall be substituted,

AWARD 

This reference arises out of Order No. L-32012/12/75- 
D-IV(A), dated 21st November, 1975 of the Ministry of 
1 aboiir, Government of India under Section 10 of the In- 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1947. The reference reads ; 

“Whether the action of the management of Messrs 
Dunnage Wood and Sciap Dealers Association, 
Calcutta in terminating the services of Shri Bacha 
Lai Choudhiiry, Watcbm.'in with effect from the 1st 
February, 1975 is justified ? If not, to what relief 
is he entitled 


Th's reference came up for final hearing on 17-3-1976. 
Though summons was received by both parties, they did 
not file any written statement, The final date of hearing 
was il o communicated to them and yet they did not turn 
up on 17 3-1976. It was posted to 18-3-1976 and again to 
19-3 1976 The parties were still absent.

try Government of India, vide No, L-19012/21 /74/D.TnB. 
dated 26th July, 1975 under Section 10 of the Industrial Dis- 
putes Act, 1947. The reference reads: 

“Whether the management of Pandaveshwar Colliery of 
Messrs Coal Mines Authority Limited, Post Office 
Pandaveshwar, District Durdwan, are justied in stop- 
ping from work Sarva Shri (i) Puran Das, (ii) Jittan 
Cyan, (iii) Sukhu Majhi, (iv) Sulamon Sk., (v) Khara 
Majhi, (vi) Chowan Majhi, (vii) Garib Majhi, (viii) 
Diba Majhi, (ix) Nizam Mia and (x) Mahadeb Das. 
Stopping Mazdoor with effect from 29th March. 
1974; If not, to what relief arc the said workmen 
entitled 7"

2. Summons to the parlies were despatched on 18-8-1975. 
The Coal Mines Authority neVnowIcdged the suinnions on 
22-8-1975. Aftciwaids Ihcy applied lo this Tiibunal nioio 
than 8 (eight) times for ex.tensioii of lime for lilinx written 
statement. But they never filed one. The Union, however, 
filed their written statement. 

3. Another summons was issued to the Coal Mines Autho- 
rity posting the reference to this date for peremptoiy hearing. 
Tltey did not turn up. So they were dcclaicd absent. 

4. On behalf of the Union its Organising Secretary Sri 
Ranjan Das Gupta was examined. He proved the claim 
against the employer. 

5. In the re.sult an award is passed in favour of all the 
workmen mentioned in the Schedule leinstating them to their 
respective posts as Mazdoors as on the date of their Slopping 
of work with back wages and all other benefits which they 
arc entitled.

.S.O. 1428. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Goveni- 
ment Industrial Tribunal. Calcutta in the indirstrial dispute 
between the employers in relation to the New Kenda Colliery 
of Messrs. Coal Mines Authority Limited, and their workmen 
which was received by the Central Government on the 23id 
March, 1976. 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL 
AT CALCUTTA 

Reference No. 49 of 1975 

PRESENT ; 

Justice H. K, Moidu, Presiding Olficcr. 

PARTIES ; 

Employers in relation to the Now Kenda Colliery of 
Messrs. Coal Mines Authority Limited. 

AND 

Their Workmen. 

APPEARANCFS : 

On behalf of Employcis — Sri Nikhiles Das, Advocate, with 
Sri M. Ghosh Chaudhnri. Group Personnel Officer. 
New Kenda Sub-Area, Area V. 

On behalf of Workmen — Sii Basudev Puitandi. 

STATE ; West Bengal. INDUSTRY ; Coal Mine. 

AWARD 

This reference arises out of the Order of the Ministry of 
Labour. Government of India vide No. L-19012/2/75/D ITI B 
^tfed 23rd July, 1975, under Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947. The reference reads : 


“Whether Ihe action of the management of New Kenda 
Colliery of Messrs, Coal Mines Authority Limited, 
Post Office 1 oposi, District Buidw.m, in retiring Shii 
N. N. Puitandy, Electrician, with eflcct from 1st 
August, 1973 is legal and justified ? If not, to what 
relief is the said workman entitled 7” 

2. The case of the Coal Mines Authority Limited is that 
the workman Shri N. N. Paifundi retired from service on 
attainment of superannuation with effect from 1-8-1973. And 


[Part T1— - 


that he had now put foiwaid a f.ilsc claim that his ago 
was wrongly assessed and that the correct year of his birth 
was 1918 Accoidin^ to them the correct year of his birth 
was 1910. till attainment of mote than 63 years of age, 
the workman retired on receipts of his provident fund and 
gratuity, Ext. M-1 was his Service card and Ext. M-2 was 
his identity card. In both those records the year of birth 
of the workman was shown as 1910. So, they slate that the 
workman had been correctly and legally retired from service. 
The Coal, Mines Authority filed a written statement as early 
as on 3-9-1975 raising these contentions. 

3. The woikman did not file any written statement in the 
case, though he received the summons in the case in August, 
1975. When the ease came up for hearing today after a 
notice was issued to the workman posting the case for peremp- 
tory hearing, he came with an application stating that the 
case .should be adjourned to another date. 1 find no subs- 
tantial ground for an adjournment. So the case was tried 
taking the evidence of the Coal Mines Authority’s witness aa 
MW-1 and that of the evidence of the workman as WW-1. 
The Coal Mines Authority produced some documents which 
were marked as Exts. M-1 to Ext. M 4 and the workman’s 
documents were marked us Exts. W 1 to W 6. 

4. The main question that arises for determination is whe- 
ther the workman has proved his correct age in order to 
ascertain whether his dalii of superammation was correctly fixed 
by the Cord Mines Authority. 

5. It is admitled that the dale of his superannuation shall 
be on his attainment of 58 years of age. The records of 
Ihe Employer show that the year of his birth was 1910. 
The workman himself was a party to Exts. Ml to M3, He 
received his provident fund amount and gratuity after his 
retirement in full discharge of all his claims against the 
employer. Ext, M-3 was his application foi- getting the Pro- 
vident fund amount. He signed it on 3-5-1974. After going 
through all these piocesses he was alleged to have got Ext. 
W-2 certificate fiom the Headmaster of a Government aided 
High School of Ethora, Burdwan District, on 29-9-1973, 
The genuineness of Ext. W-2 was questioned by the employer 
vide Ext. M-4 letter. That was never answered by the 
Headmaster. The workman has not taken any step to prove 
tile authenticity of Ext. W-2. At one sta.qe of his examina- 
tion he stated that he has no personal knowledge of any 
such register maintained in the High School. The othei docu- 
ments produced by the workman do not help him to fix his 
correct age. When there are admitted documents in the 
custody of the Employer rcgaiding the correctness of the 
age of the workman, it would be difficult lo rely upon the 
workman’s documents which are not proved to establish his 
age. f find no re.ason to reject the documents or the evidence 
adduced on behalf of the management. I hold that the cor- 
rect ajte of the workman had been fixed and he was allowed 
to retnc in correct time with effect from 1st August, 1973. 

6. In the icsull, the reference is answered in favour of 
the management and against the workman 

An award is passed accordingly. 

Dated, Calcutta, 

The 17th March. 1976. 


F. K. MOIDU, Presiding Officer

THF GAZETl'E OF INDIA : APRIL 17, 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 



Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : 


BEFORR THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRrAL 

TRIBGNAL NO. 2, DHANBAD 
In the malter of a reference under Section 10(l)(d) of the 

Industrial Di.spiites Act, 1947 

PRESENT ; 

Shii K. K. Surkai- — Presiding Officer. 

Reference No. 90 of 1975 

(Ministry’s Order No. L-2901 1 /92/75/D. IfTB, dt. 23-70975) 
PARTIES : 

Employers in relation to the management of Malpahari 
Mnjooikola Stone Mine of Messrs. All India Stone 
Company, Pakur. 

AND 

Their Worynen 

APPEARANCES : 

For the Employers.^ — Shri S. Ganguly. 

For the Workmen — None. 

STA'I E ; Bihar. INDUSTRY : Stone Mine. 

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, sent the 
above reference to this Tribunal for adjudication of the 
industrial dispute involved with the following issues framed : — 

“(1) Whether the action of the management of Malpahari 
Majoorkola Stone Mine of Messrs All India Stone 
Company, Pakur in terminating the services of Shri 
Jayanti Kumar Banerjee, Bluster, in the Malpahari 
Majoorkola Stone Mine, with effect from the 31st 
January, 1975, on the ground of unfitness due to 
old age is justified ? 

(2) If not, to what relief is. the said workman entitled T’ 

2. On receipt of the above order of reference notices were 
issued to both sides. The employers All India Stone Com- 
pany, Malpahari Majoorkola Stone Mine, appeared before this 
Tribunal through their authorised representative. None atv 
peared for the workmen b,ut a petition was received on Sib 
October, 1975 from the President of Stotic Quarry Workers’ 
Union, Pakur praying for time for an indefinite period in 
view of emergency. The Tribunal was not in a position 
to grant him an indefinite adjournment on the pica as taken 
by the President of the Union representing the workmen. 
The workmen were, therefore, directed to lllc written state- 
ment and complete other steps, failing which the case would 
be disposed of according to law in the absence of the work- 
man without further notice. Then the case was fixed on 
7-11-1975 on which date the authorised representative of the 
employers appeared and prayed for some time to filo their 
written statement. None was present for the workmen nor 
any step was taken by them, The case was next fixed on 
1-12-1975 when the anthorised representative of the employers 
appeared and filed written statement. This time also none 
was present for the workmen nor any step was taken. Then 
the case was again fixed on 5-1-76 and 16-2-76 when none 
was present for the workman nor any step was taken by 
them. It will, therefore, appear that since the date of order 
of reference in July, 1975 lill 16-2-1976 the workmen or 
their representative were never present nor took any step in 
the case. On the last date fixed for hearing a repicscnlative 
from the employers appeared. He submitted that it was 
the workmen who raised the industrial dispute and the posi- 
tion of the employers was like that of a defendant to the claim 
of the workmen. In the absence of the workmen all through, 
the employers do not have any industrial dispute subsisting 
and they are not interested lo proceed with this case any 
more. There is no reason whv the workmen should not take 
any step all thiough Ihc pendency of this reference. I am, 
therefore, inclined to suppose that the workmen arc no longer 
interested lo proceed with this case as they have no industrial 
dispute subsisting at the present moment. There is no indi- 
cation that the workmen would be coming for their case within 


APRIL 17, 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 1509 


a reasonable period. The inrlustiial dispute referred to have 
thus not been pressed for adjudication by either side. So 
the matter boils down to this that the parties have no longer 
any industrial dispute subsisting. T have, therefore, no other 
option but to pass a ‘no dispute award’ in this case. 

3. In the result. I make a ‘no dispute award’ in respect of 
this instant Reference. 

Dhanbad, dated, the I9thi March, 1976, 

K. K, SARKAR, Presiding Officer 

[No. L-2901 l/92/75-D-III(B)l

AND

APPEARANCES : 

For the Employees — Shri S. Ganguly.

STATE: Bihar. INDUSTRY; Stone Mine. 


AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, sent the 
above reference to this Tribunal for adjudication of the indus- 
trial dispute involved with the following issues framed : — 

“(1) Whether 'the action of the management of Malpahari 
Majoorkola Stone Mine of Messrs. All India Stone 
Company, Pakur in terminating the services of Shri 
Shyama Kunai, Machine Mazdoor, in Malpahari 
Majoorkola Slone Mine with effect from 27th Janu- 
ary, 1975 is justified 7 

(2) Tf not, to what relief is the said workman entitled 7” 

2. On receipt of the above order of reference notices were 
issued to both sides. The employers All India Stone Company, 
Malpahari Majoorkola Stone Mine, appeared before this Tri- 
bunal through their authorised representaive. None appeared 
for the workmen but a petition was received on 8-10-1975 
from the President of Stone Quarry Workers’ Union, Pakur 
playing tor time for an indefinite period in view of emergency. 
The Tribunal was not in a position lo grant him an indefinite 
adjournment on the plea as taken by the President of the 
Union representing the workmen. The workmen were, there- 
fore, directed to file written statement and complete other 
steps, lailing which the case would be disposed of according 
to law in the absence of the woikmen without further notice. 
Then the case was fixed on 7-11-1975 on which date the 
authorised rcpreseiitalive of the employers appeared and pray- 
ed for some time to file their written statement. None was 



1510 THF GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 17, 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 [Part II— 


present foi the workmen nor any sten was taken by them. 
The case was next fixed on 1-12-1975 when the authorised 
representative ol the employers appeared and filed written 
statement. This time also none was present for the workmen 
nor any step was taken. Then the case was again fixed on 
5-1-76 and 16-2-76 when none was present for the workmen 
nor any step was taken by them. It will, therefore, appear 
that since the date of order of reference in July, 1975 till 
16-2-1976 the workmen or their representative were never 
present nor took any step in the case. On the last date fixed 
for hearing a representative Irom the employers appeared. 
He submitted that it was the workmen who raised the indus- 
trial dispute and the position of the employers was like that 
of a defendant to the claim of the woikmen, Tn the absence 
of the workmen all through, the employers do not have any 
industrial dispute subsisting and they ate not interested to 
proceed with this case any more, There is no reason why 
the workmen should not take any step all through the pen- 
dency of this reference, 1 am, thcrefote, inclined to suppose 
that the workmen are no longer interested to proceed with 
this case as they have no industrial dispute subsisting at the 
present moment. There is no indication that the workmen 
would be coming for their case within a reasonable period. 
The industiral dispute referred to have thus not been pressed 
for adjudication by cither side. So the matter boils down to 
this that the parties have no longer any industrial dispute 
SLibsisling. I have, therefore, no other option but to pass 
a ‘no dispute award’ in this case. 

3. In (he result, 1 make a ‘no dispute award’ in respect of 
this instant Reference. 

K. K. SARKAR, Presiding Officer. 
Dhanbad, dated, the 19th March, 1976.

putes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby 
publishes the following award of the Central Government 
Industrial Tiibunal, Calcutta, In the industrial dispute between 
the employci's in relation to the management of South Parasea 
Colliery of Messrs. South Parasea Colliery Private Limited and 
their woikmen, which was leceived by the Central Govern- 
ment on the 1st April, 1976. 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDLSTRIAL TRIBUNAL 
AT CALCUTTA 

PRESENT : 

Justice E K. Moidti, Presiding Officer. 

Reference No 110 of 1971 

PARTIES : 

Employcis in relation to the management of ^uth Paia- 
sea Colliery of Messrs South Parasea Colliery Private 
Limited, 

AND 

Their Workmen. 

APPEARANCES ; 

On behalf of Employe] s — Absent. 

On behalf of Workmen — Sri Satyen Bancrjee, Advocate. 
STATE ; West Bengal. INDUSTRY : Coal Mine. 

AWARD 

By Ordei No. L/1912/93/71-LR11, dated Iblfc October, 
1971, the then Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation (De- 
partment of Laboui & Employment), Government of India, 
a dispute between the management of South Parasea Colliery 
of Messis. South Parasea Colliery Private Limited and their 
workmen was leferred to this Tiibunal imdei Section 10 of 
the Industiial Disputes Act, 1947, foi adjudication. The refer- 
ence reads : 

“1, Whether the action of the management of South Para- 
sea Colliciy of Messrs South Parasea Colliery Pri- 


vate Limited, Post Office Kajoragram, District 
Burdwan in laying off the workmen without compen- 
sation with effect from the 29th May, 1971, to the 
27th June, 1971, was justified If not, to what relict 
are the workmen entitled ? 


2. Whether the forced unemployment of the workmen 
of .South Paiasea Collieiy of Messrs South Parasea 
Colliery Private Eimiled, Post Office Kajoragram, 
District Burdwan by the management with effect 
from the 28th June, 1971, is justified? If not. to 
what relief the workmen concerned are entitled ?” 


2. This tribunal recorded a finding on a preliminary issue 
inter sc between the two unions. One of them took up the 
matter to the Calcutta High Court in a writ proceedings under 
Article 226 of the Constitution. On the dale of hearing nci- 
their party was present at the High. Court and hence the writ 
petition had been dismissed. The matter has come back to 
this Tribunal for disposal of the rctcrencc in accordance with 
law. 

3. Notice of the reference was .sent to the Manager, South' 
Paiascu Colliery, P.O. Kajoragram, Disl, Burdwan, as welt as 
to the Coal Mines Authority, as employers. The previous 
employer did not turn up and contest the claim. The Coal 
Mines Aiithorily contended lhat they are not liable for any 
of Ihc claims made by Ihe workmen in the case. The claim 
aiosc long before the nationalisation of the coal mines came 
into existence. So ihe Coal Mines Aulhoiily is not liable 
for the amount to be fixed as payable to the woikmen. 


4. On the two points raised in the reference there is only 
the evidence of one workman examined as witness No. 1. 
His evidence established that the workmen are entitled to com- 
pensation in view of the lay off with effect from 29th May, 
1971 ending with 271h June, 1971. The period does nol 
exceed 45 days. The evidence of the workman has to bo 
accepted in the absence of any other counter-evidence. I 
find that the workmSn would be entitled to compensation 
undei the provisions of Sec. 25C of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947. All Ihe workmen involved would be paid 50 
per cent of the total of basic wa^e and dearness allowance 
during the period when the lay off was in foice. The point 
1 is answered accordingly. 


5. On the second point, here again the evidence of the 
witness has to be accepted that the woikmen were not per- 
mitted to work during the pciiod beginning fiom 28th June. 
1971 to 18th August, 1971 when the coal mine was said to 
have reopened. The workmen claim compensation for this 
pciiod. But the evidence docs not establish that the work- 
mens’ claim comes either under Sec. 25F or under Sec, 25FFE. 
So, the workmen would be entitled to actual wages to which 
they would be entitled for the period from 28th June, 1971 
to I8th Augustj 1971. The amount so calculated as their 
total wages during the pci iad shall be paid to them. 

6. In the result, an award is passed in favour of the work- 
men of South Parasea Colliery givinjj (hem compensation 
equal to 50 per cent of their total basic wages and deal ness 
allowance from 29th May, 1971 to 27th June, 1971 and full 
wages to each of the workman from 28th June. 1971 to 
I7th August, 1971 and the amount so fixed shall be paid by 
the erst-whilc management of the South Parasea Colliery 
of Messrs, South Parasea Colliciy Private Limited Post Office 
Kajoragram, District Burdwan. 


Dated, Calcutta, E. K. MOIDU, Presiding Officer 

The 25th March, 1976.

AWARD 

The Government of India in Ministry of Labour throng 
notification No. L-21012/14/74-LR.II/D.II1B dated 3rd April, 
1975 referred the Industiial Dispute between the employer 
in relation to Ramagundam Division of Singareni Collieries 
Company Limited, .Todavari Khani, Karimnagar (A.P.) and 
their Workman under Sections 7A and 10(l)(d) of the Indiw- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 (which would hereinafter be called 
the Act) lor adjudication by the Tribunal on the following 
issue : 

‘’Whelher Ihe action of the management of Singareni 
Collieries Company Limited, Ramagundam Division, 
Godavari Khani Post, Karimnagar District, Andhra 
Pradesh, in not promiting and confirming Shri T. 
Agaiah, Electrician as Category V Electrician is 
justified V If not, to what relief is the concerned 
workman entitled ?" 

2. The reference was registered as Industrial Dispute 
No. 14 of 1975 and notices wore directed to the workman 
and to the employer. In the counter filed by the workman 
it is inter alia alleged llial he was appointed as Electrician 
in No. 6 Incline, Godavari Khani by the Respondent- 
Management in Novemb'.r, 1962. He was promoted to 
Category IV Electrician in 1964. He was entrusted with 
complete charge of the shift since December 1972 and was 
being paid .Acting allowance of Category V Electrician ever 
since, It is contended that by the time the dispute wa.s taken 
up for conciliation he had put in a service of 12 years as 
Electrician and was also paid Acting Allowance continuously 
for two years in thal category of Electrician V. Since the 
time he was entrusted independent charge of the duties of 
Category V Electrician he has been discharging his duties 
satisfactorily. As per the Tradesmen Agreement dated 
4-10-1966 there is said to be a clear vccancy of Category 

V Electrician in this Incline but the services of this work- 
man were being utilised by the employer-respondent only 
by paying the Acting Allowance, without confirming the 
claimant in that post, even though it is a clear vacancy. 
When this claimant requested for confirmation in Category 

V the Management is said to have refused to confirm him 
on the ground that he did not hold requisite qualifications 
of Trade Certificate from I.T.L, as per the Company’s 
Circular dated 10-6-1970. It is, however, admitted by him 
that the claimant does not hold a certificate of I.T.I But 
what is contended is that as per the Tradesmen Agreement 
dated 4-10-1966 there was no condition that only I.T.I. 
certificate holders will be eligible for promotion for Category 

V Electrician. U is alleged that even after the Circular 
dated 10-6-1970 the Maiiagemcnl promoted certain Electri- 
cians who were not holding LT.l. certificates, to Categories 

V and VI, Sri Abraham is said to have been promoted from 
Category V to Category VI. Nalli Rayalingam is said to have 
been similarly promoted from Category V to Category VI. 
Sri Shankar is said to h.ive been promoted similarly from 
Category V to VI. Certain other instances are also given 
by the claimant. These persons are said lo have been pro- 
moted in the year 197.f. But the claimant was unjustly re- 
fused confirmation in Category V in.spitc of the fact that 


for more than two ytars his services have been utilised as 
Category V Electrician. On 17-4-1975 the Management is 
said to have issued ancthcr Circular whcrc-under even 
though the persons in seivicc do not possess I.T.I. certificates 
the Hleclrician and I'itterr are held eligible for promotion 
lo higher categories on passing the Tiade Test. In view of 
his long service of 13 years and also having discharged the 
duties of Vth Category Electrician and in view of this post 
being a clear vacancy, il 's prayed that he may be confirmed 
as Category V Electrician. 

3. In Ihe cotinler (’led by the Management it is admitted 
that the claimant was appointed on 5-11-1962 and was pro- 
moted as Electrician on 7-6-1964. On implementation of 
the Wage Board Recommendations he was placed in new 
Category IV with elfcct from 15-8-1967. It is also admitted 
that whenever the claimant was asked to ofiiciate as Category 

V Electrician he was paid allowances. 11 is, however, alleged 
that the claimant is not efficient and is, therefore, not 
eligible for promotion to Category V (Electrician). The 
General Manager is said to have issued a Circular on 
10-6-1970 prescribing the qualification for an Electrician or 
Eitter, but the Claimant is said to have no .such qualifications 
to be confirmed in Category V. Minutes as per this Circular 
arc said to have been drawn on 29-5-1972 which were also 
agreed by the Union of Ihc workman, It is thus contended 
that the claimant is tot entitled to be promoted to Category 

V as he has no qualification and efficiency, It is alleged 
that in Godavari Khani 6th Incline, there is a Category V 
Electrician in each shift and therefore, i( is denied that the 
claimant was entrusted with the independent charge of 
Category V Electrician or that he acted in that capacity 
from 1972 onwards. It is contended if any Electrician was 
promoted to Category V after the issue of Circular dated 
10-6-1970 it is only on proof of his efficiency. It is also 
alleged that a.s per the Circular dated 17-4-1975 Electricians 
with long service of more than 13 years and who passed the 
Trade test condticied by the Management are eligible to 
be promoted to Category V. The claimant is said to have 
neither long service nor did he pass the Trade Test, That 
apart, promotion is alleged to be a managerial function. 
Thus the claim of the claimant is said to be not justified, 

4. The workman e.vamined himself in support of his claim 
as W'.W.l. He also relied upon Exs.Wl lo W6, Ex.Wl 
relates to the procedure to be followed wilh regard to the 
grievances of Inc workman. As per the procedure laid down 
therein, the workman made his representation for confirming 
him in Category V to the Manager, to the .Agent, and to 
the Chairman of the Grievance Committee. The Manager 
as per Ex.W3 replied tljai ihc ca.se of the workman tor 
promotion cannot be considered as his atlcndance is irregu- 
lar and his work is not upto Ihc mark. This letter is dated 
27th January, 1974. Under Ex W5 the same view was 
communicated by the Agent rejecting his claim for prL>- 
niotion. It does not appear that the Chairman of the Grie- 
vance Committee has inlitnated the workman inspitc of the 
latter's leprescntation to him as per Ex.W6. The management 
in rebuttal examined two witnesses in oral evidence ns 
M.W.l and M.W,2 and lelicd upon Exs. Ml to M4. M.W. 1 
IS the Divisional Supciinlendcni at present at Bellampalli 
Division but was the Manager of Godavari Khani No.6 
Incline from 1968 Octobci lo 15th December 1974. M.W.2 
is the Superintending Mining Engineer of Incline No, 6 
for the lust one year and lour moiilhs. 

5. II is common case that from 1964 the claimant was 
working as Category IV Electrician. Consequent to the im- 
plementation of the Wage Board recommendations from 
15-8-1967 he was assigned new Category IV Electrician. 
Even from the ver.sion of the Management as borne ont 
by F.X.M3 the extract of payment of Acting Allowance, the 
claimant has been .icting in Vth Category from December, 
1972. The controversy here is that according to the claimant 
he has been continnously acting in thal category from 
December 1972 to March 1975, in which month according 
to the claimanl, one Odelki was promoted from Category 
IV to V and posted in his place It is, however, admitted by 
M.W.2, that there are three shifts and one general shift in 
6th Incline and that duiing the period from 1972 to 1975 
there were only two regular Category V Electricians and 
this required two acting Category V Electiicians. I| is also 
adiniiled by bim that die diiim.nil was ollici.iling biU what is 
.sought to be csiablished is that he was not working conti- 
nuously. Il is, however, the very .admission of M.W.2 that 
whenever the claimant was put in the first shift or the general 
shift no Acting Allowance was given lo him. Thus Ex.M3 



1512 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 17, 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 [Part li- 


the extract of payment of allowance docs not represent the 
total number of days worked by this claimant in Category 
V. It is, however, seen that for the year 1973 the claimant 
according to M.W 2 was paid allowance for 234 days out 
of 305 working days in tfiat vear. Since Ex. M3 does not 
represent the correct position of the total number of days, 
for the reasons stated above, it can as well be stiid that the 
claimant continuoiislv worked during that year. Even from 
the version of M.W 2 in the absence of any other Electri- 
cian and four such Electricians being i-equired one for each 
shift per day it is reasonable to hold that the claiment has 
been acting in that post as per the requirement of the 
Management. Thus there is no difficulty in holding, as claim- 
ed by the claimant that from December, 1972 to March 
1975 he has been working continiiosuly in that post. It is 
also an admitted fact that he was appointed in the year 
1962 and by this time he has put in l3 years of service. 

The only point is that whether in view of his long service 

and also his officialion in Category V for a period of more 
than two years, is he eligible for promotion or not. 

6. The Management issued Ex. Ml as to the conditions 
of promotion as early as Junf 1970. The qualifications for 
promotion or appointment to Category V Electrician as 
per this notificalion ate an Electrician Certificate grunted by 
the Stale Government of Andhra Prudesh or other equivalent 
recognised certificate. Preference is said to be given to per- 
sons who have under gone I.T.I. course and possess the 

Electrician Certificate, They should also pass the Trade 

Test for Category V conducted by the Company. It would 
transpire from the evidence of M.W.l and Ex. M2 dated 
17th December, 1970 that the claimant was sent for Trade 
Test in the year 1970 uut he did not pass. It is also stated 
In Ex. M2 that the work of the claimant was also nol satis- 
factory and therefore his case for promotion cannot be 
considered. It is also deposed by M.W.l that subsequent to 
that dale the claimant was not sent for appearing for any 
test. It is admitted by the workman also that those who are 
not having l.T.l. certificate are promoted after the test of 
Iheir efficiency and that this was being done from the year 
1970. What his grievance is. that he should have been equal- 
ly given (he opportunity of appearing for a test, But this 
be was deprived of. It is also conceded by the workman 
after 1972 a written test is also prescribed. The workman 
would also put it that he is ready to appear for any effi- 
ciency test. Though the Management would put it that the 
work of this claimant in Category V was not satisfactory, 
there is nothing to show that it was so. The Management 
has not filed any record to show that on any prior occa- 
sion the claimant was issued any Memo which would indi- 
cate that his performance was not satisfactory. The Manage- 
ment would also allege that the claimant was not regular 
in his attendance. The workman as W.W.l would allege 
that even for one day’s late attendance he was being charge 
sheeted. The irregularities in attendance could as well be 
the matter of some action by the Management under the 
Standing Orders. But that is far from saying that the work 
or performance of the claimant is not satisfactory. At any 
rate the workman has been continuously officiating for more 
than two years in that category. If his performance rc,sultcd 
in any loss or break out of fire as is sought to be painted 
by the Management, there could have been some written 
record of those occasions when the workman could be said 
guilty of lapses. In the absence of any record filed by the 
Management 1 am constrained to hold that the allegation 
about the unsatisfactory performance of the claimant is 
devoid of truth. It is not only the case of the Management 
that the non-T.T.I. personnel are promoted only after a trade 
lest but also admitted by the workman that from 1970 such 
tests of efficiency are imposed. It is also the admission of the 
workman that after 1972 written tests arc also prescribed. 
Admittedly (he workman does not possess any T.T.T. quali- 
fication or the certificate of an Electrician but that in view 
of his long service and also his experience as Category V 
Electrician, the Management ought to have given him an 
opportunity even under the Circular dated 17-4-1975. It is 
true that these tests are conducted as depo.sed by M.W.l ns 
and when the vacancies arose. Hut as admitted by M.W. 1 
the claimant was sent for test in the year 1970 and that too 
only once. It is relevant to record that he officiated in Cate- 
gory V from December 1972 long after that alleged test. 
The officialion for a peiiotl of two years or more in that 
category would have afforded sufficieni oppoitunity to the 
claimant to acquaint himself with the duties of Category V 
Electrician. As alleged in the counter even as per the Circu- 
lar dated 17-4-1975 Electricians having more than 13 years 


of service and passing the Trade Test are admitted to be 
eligible for promtions. The claimant has by now put in 13 
years of service and if an opportunitw is given he may prove 
his efficiency for being promoted. Though the tests are 
not held at regular intervals, and such tests being only neces- 
sary in the interest of the working ol the establishment, and 
that being a regular feature, all that can be said is that the 
claimant has to equally go through such a tc.st. Though it 
is alcgel by the claimant that persons like Abraham, Raya- 
lingain and Shankar were promoted though they do not pos- 
sess any I.T.I. qualifications, the Management has come for- 
ward with a letter dated 31 -8-1972 (an unmarked docu- 
ment) to show that Abraham was piomoted as a Fitter in 
Category VT from Category V only on the basis of his pas- 
sing the Ti'ade Test, The case with regard to Rayalingam 
appears to be the same. Shankar is also promoted on passing 
the Trade Test. It cannot theiefore, be said that while other 
Electricians of Category V were promoted this claimant was 
in any way discriminated. Thus passing of the Trade test 
as laid down by the Management is a nccc,ssary condition 
for promotion. The only grievance the claimant epn make 
in the circumstance is that he should also be sent for such 
a test and given an opportunity to prove his cllioiency. As 
noted above, even as per the Circular 17-4 -975 the clai- 
mant has put in 13 years of service by this date. The only 
other condition foi- his promotion is that he should pass the 
Trade test. The claimant rightly makes grievance of the fact 
that unless he is scut for such a lest he cannot by himself take 
it. 'I'hus, in the circumstances, the only direction to the 
Management would be to send this claimant for a test with- 
in a period of six months. I am sure that in view of his ex- 
perience the workman would be competent to prove his 
efficiency and would pass the test. 1 am equally hopeful that 
the Management unimindfttl of this litigation would give 
him a fair chance and would consider his case being un-in- 
flucnced by this litigation and without any prejudice. Though 
the reference is answered holding that the non-con- 
confirmation of the workman, by this time cannot be said 
to be unjustified in view of his not passing the Trade Test for 
no fault of his, that finding is subject to the observations 
(hat the Respondent-Management would send the claimant- 
workman for a Trade test within six months from this date 
and thus give him the opportunity of proving his efficiency 
and thu.s qualify himself for promolion lo Category V, 

Award passed accordingly.

S.O. 1433. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
lhat an indnstrial dispute exists between the employers in 
relation to the management of the Western Railway Car- 
riage Workshops Canteen, Parcl and Mahalaxmi, Bombay, 
and their workmen in re.spcct of the matters specified in the

New Delhi, the 31st March. 1976 

S.O. 1437. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby published the following award of the arbitrator in 
the industrial dispute between the employers in relation to 
the management of the Cantonment Board, Ambala Canton- 
ment and their workmen, which was received by the Cen- 
tral Government on the 27th March, 1976 : — 



Sec. 3(ii)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 17, 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 


1515 


ARBITRATION AWARD GIVEN BY SHRl J. L. WADHI, 
ASSISTANT LABOUR COMMISSIONER (CENTRAL) 
KANPUR AND ARBITRATOR UNDER SECTION 
lOA OF THE INDUSTRIAL DISPUTES ACT. 1947 
IN THE MATTER OF DISPUl'E BETWEEN THE 
MANAGEMENT OF CANTONMENT BOARD, 
AMBALA AND THEIR WORKMAN REPRESENTED 
BY ALL INDIA CANTONMENT BOARD EMPLO- 
YEE:S FEDERATION, AMBALA CANTT. 


PRESENT : Shri J. L. Wadhi, Assisliint Labour Commissioner 
(C) & Arbitrator, Kanpur. 

Representing the Management ; 

Shri K. R, A. N. Ayer, Executive Officer, Cantonment 
Board, Ambala Cantt. 

Representing the Workmen : 

Shri J. D. Bakshi, General Secretary, All India Cantt. Board 
Employees Federation, Ambala Cantt. 

The Executive Officer, Cantonment Board, Ambala and 
All India Cantonment Board Eihployees Federation, Ambala 
Cantt by an agreement dated 30th June, 1975 agreed to 
refer an Industrial Dispute existing between them over the 
issue of alleged denial of proper scale of pay i.e. Rs. 400 — 
1100 to Dr. C. R. Bhatlacharjee w.e.f. 1-2-1969 to my 
arbitration under Section lOA of the Industrial Disputes Act. 
1947. The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour 
in persuance of the provision of Section 10A(3) of the In- 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1947 published the said arbitration 
agreement vide Notificatiodn No. L-13012/2/75-D.IIB dated 
the 23rd July, 1975. The parties extended the lime for giv- 
ing _my award by another period of three months. The fol- 
lowing specific matter in dispute was referred to my arbi- 
tration. 

"Whether the action of the management of the Canton- 
ment Board, Ambala in denying Dr. C. R. Bhattacharjeo the 
scale of pay of Rs. 400 — 1100 w.e.f. 1-2-1969 is legal and 
justified 7 If not, to what relief is he entiOed 7". 

The parties were asked to file their written statement of 
claims and accordingly the written statement of the work- 
man was received by me on 7th August, 1975 and the raana- 
pement was received on 25th October 1975. The hearing 
in this case was held on 23-9-1975 when the management 
desired to file their written arguments within a fortnight. 
The same were received on 25th October, 1975. The final 
hearing lin this case was held on 12-12-1975. 

The case of Dr. C. R. Bhattacharjec is that he is emp- 
loyed by the management of Cantonment Board, Ambala 
for the last 10 years as an Assistant Surgeon. He was ap- 
pointed in the grade of Rs. 250 — ^550 with 5 advance in- 
crements keeping in view with hiis past experience and at 
present is placed in the revised grade of R.s. 350 — 600. 

The union’s contention is that Dr. Bhattacheijec was ap- 
pointed j‘n the grade of Rs. 250^ — 550 and according to 
the National Tribunal Award the rules of the State Govern- 
ment are applicable to the employees of Cantonment Board 
as far as the pay scale are concerned. The State Govern- 
ment has revised the grade of Rs, 250—550 to Rs. 400— 
1100 for their employees and according to them Dr. 
Bhattacharjee is eligible for the grade of Rs. 400 — 1100 
as he has more than 10 years service at his credit. 

In support of this union has made reliance on (be letter 
of Director of Health Service, Haryana’s letter No. F.-n-2-71 
876/2443 dated 27-1-1971 copy of which they have filed 
along with their written statemert The main list of the let- 
ter M as under: — 

“The qualifications prescribed for requirement to H.C.M. 
Qass n in the scale of pay of Rs. 400 — 30 — 700/4 — 1100 
are as under 

1. M.B.B.S. 

2. Registered with Punjab Medical Council. 

25 per cent vacancies in the cadre of H.M.C. II are fill 
led by promotion on the basis of seniority cum-mcrit from 
5 GI/76— 7 


A.M.O.'s having 5 years permanent service at their credit for 
whom the qualification for recruitment is medical licenciate." 

The management contended that Dr. Bhattacharjee was 
working as Assistant Surgeon in their hospital at Ambala 
and Was appointed in the scale of Rs. 250—550 with live 
increments. The scale for M.B.B.S. doctors at that time was 
Rs. 250—750 and the grade given to Dr. Bhattacharjee was 
that of a licenciate doctor. Later on Dr. Bhattacharjee was 
given the revised scale of Rs. 350—600 as applicable to 
Haryana State for L.S.M.F. as per National Tiiipunal Award. 
Since the AwartI was fully implemented, the grade of 400— 
1100 can not be given to Dr. Bhatta^arjee. The manage- 
ment filed two letters issued by the Director of Health ^r- 
viccs, Haryana in support of their case. The jist of one 
letter dated 25-2-1974 is as under : — 

“The promotion of A.M.O. to the post of Medical Offi- 
cer Is not automatic on the completion of 12 years service. 
It is subject to other factors such as availability of vacancies 
in H.C.M.S.-H, quota, past performance, seniority etc. for 
the purpose of counting 12 years continuous service, for 12 
years it has to be in the cadre of A.M.O. under Punjab/ 
Haryana Government. 

The second letter dated 27-1-1971 referred to by the 
management Ls the same which the union has referrrf and 
the jist has aleady been reported earlier. The management 
further stated that Dr. Bhattacharjee does not come under 
the quota of 25 pw cent and also there was no vacancy as 
such the claim is incorrect and untenable. 

In view of the contentions put forward by both the par- 
ties it is to be seen whether Dr, Bhattacharjee is eligible for 
the scale of Rs, 400 — 1100 or nol7 Tlic parties have stated 
that Dr. Bhattacherjec is a licenciate doctor and both the 
parties have made reliance on the letters issued by the 
Director of Health Services, Haryana State. From these let- 
ters it will be seen that the grade of Rs. 400 — 1100 is 
given to M.B.B.S. Doctor on direct recruitment and to a 
licenciate doctor with 12 years service at his credit on pro- 
motion, In the letter produced by the union the licenciate 
Doctor with 5 years services at his credit is eligible for pro- 
motion to this grade. The point is whether Dr. Bhattacherjec 
can be eligible to the grade automatically or other pre-requi- 
sit are essential to get his grade. 

Both letters of the Director of Health Services lays down 
hat the grade of Rs. 400 — 1100 can be given to the 
licenciate doctor on promotion. The other requirements is 
that there should be a post against w'hicb one should be 
coMidered for promotion. In this case there was no post for 
which Dr. Bhattacherjee can be considered for promotion and 
the pre-requisits are not fnlffiled as such Dr. Bhattacharjee 
could not be considered for promotion and is not eligible 
for the grade of Rs. 400—1100. 

In view of the above facts I find that the action of the 
management in denying the grade of Rs. 400 — 1100 to 
Dr. Bhattacherjee is justified and he Is not entitle to any re- 
lief, The parties will bear their own cost. 

Award is passed accordingly. 

J. L, WADHI, Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central) 
Kanpur, the 25th March, 1976

hereby publishes the following award of the Industrial Tri- 
bunal, Hyderabad in the industrial dispute between the emp- 
loyers in relation to the management of the Nuclear Fuel 
Complex, Hyderabad and their workman, which was received 
by the Central Government on the SOth March, 1976. 



1516 


THE GAZEITE OF INDIA : APRIL 17. 1976/CHAITRA 28, 1898 [Part II— 


BFFORE THE INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (CENTRAL) 

AT HYDERABAD 

Industrial Dispute No. 36 oT 1975 

PRESENT : 

Sri T. Narasinfi Rao, M.A., l.L.B, Industriul Tribunal. 
Hyderabad. 

BETWEEN 

Workman of Nuclear Fuel Complex, Moula Ali, 
Hyderabad. 

AND 

The Management, Nuclear Fuel Complex, Moula Ali, 
Hyderabad. 

APPEARANCES ; 

Sri I A. Naidu, Advocate — for Management. 

None present — for Workman. 

AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour, through 
Notification No. L-42012(10)/75-D. IIB dated 26th July, 1976 
referred the industrial dispute between the employers in rela- 
tion to the Management of Nuclear Fuel Complex, Moula 
Ali, Hyderabad and theii Workman, under Sections 7A and 
10(l)(d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (which would 
hereinafter be called the Act) for adjudication by the Tribunal 
on the following issue 

"Whether the m-inagement of Nuclear Fuel Complex. 
Moula Ali, Hyderabad, is Justified in treating Shri 
R. P. Kala, Helper ‘B’ to have abandoned his emp- 
loyment With ellecl from the forenoon of 5-10-1974. 
and in refuaing to take him in employment ? If 
not, to what relief is the said workman entitled ?" 

2. The reference is reglslcrcd as Industrial Dispute No. 
36 ol 1975 and notices wcie directed to the workman as well 
as to the' Management. The workman filed n claims state- 
ment, inter alia alleging that he was originally appointed 
as Watchman with effect from 4-8-1972 and later he applied 
to the post of Helper 'H' in the same establishment when a 
vacancy arose. He was thus selected and appointed as helper 
’B’ in Ziiconium Sponge Plant with effect from 8-11-1973. 
It is further alleged that on account of the effect of chlorine 
gas where the workman was working, he contacted eosinophillia 
from 5-10-1974. On 6-10-1974 he submitted his leave appli- 
cation to the Administrative Officer of the Respondent-Man- 
agement intimating it about the treatment he was under-going. 
It i.s further iillcged that he again submitted an application 
under registered acknowledgement enclosing a medical certi- 
ficate duly attested by Civil Surgeon. Osmania General Hos- 
pital. On his recovery, he reported to duty on 28-12-1974 
with a medical fitness certificate, but he was not allowed to 
join duty and he was informed that his services stood termi- 
nated .as per a letter dated 15-11-1974, It is, however, con- 
tended that the said letter was never served on him. But 
on 28-12-1974 that order was served upon him and his 
signature was obtained on a plain paper. On the same day 
he made a representation and again on 11-1-1975 he contacted 
the Administrative Officer but he rcfuscdl to accept the 
second application, and therefore, he sent it through registered 
post. Nothing is alleged in these claims statement about the 
outcome of the second petition or the representations. All 
that Is he prayed for is that the Tribunal should intervene 
in the matter and do justice to hum and to reinstate him 
with continuity of service, with back wages from 28-12-1974. 

3. In the counter filed by the Respondent, it is contended 
that the services of the claimant-workman were not terminated 
at all as it was a case of abandonment of service and loss of 
lien a.s per the Standing Orders. The reference under Section 
10 is said to be bad and thus the Tribunal has no jurisdiction 
to entertain or adjudicate the Industrial dispufe. It is admitted 
that the claimant was initially appointed as a watchman and 
was subsequently appointed as Helper ‘B’ with effect from 
15lh December, 1973 but not from 8th November, 1973 as 
alleged in the claims slatcment. is. however, admitted that 
he was posted to work in Zirconium Sponge Plant of th* 


Respondent-Management, It is emphatically denied that the 
claimant-petitioner or any other workman in the said Plant 
was affected by chlorine gas. The plant is said to have been 
designed according to international standards and based on 
exi^ricncc gained, obviating any health hazards like radio 
activity, powder flying etc. It is also contended that no other 
workmen of this Plant ever complained of any ill-effects on 
account of chlorine gas. It is denied that tnc Respondent 
ever submitted an application nor sent it through post on 
October 6. 1974. The only communication that was received 
from the workman was on 13th December, and that was a 
medical certificate dated 22nd November, 1974 issued by an 
Asst. Civil Surgeon and countersigned by the Civil Surgeon 
of the Osmania General Hospital. The dales in the said 
certificate are said to be conflicting, altered and corrected and. 
therefore, it was considered not to be a genuine certificate 
As there was no leave application Irom the claimant, on 26th 
October. 1974, on account of unauthorised absence of the 
workman from 5th October, 1974, onwards a Memo was 
sent to the claimant drawing his attention to Clause 34 of the 
Standing Orders and requiring him_ to report to duty im- 
mediately. That Memo was sent in the usual course by ordi- 
nary post. Since the said letter was not returned to the 
sender, it is alleged that Ihe claimant must have received it. 
Since the claimant had not joined inspite of this Memo and 
continued to bo absent till November 15, 1974, a letter was 
addressed by the Respondent to the claimant notifying that 
the claimant automatically lost his lien on his employment 
as per Standing Order Clause No. 34. This letter was sent 
to the address of the claimant as furnished by him in his 
earlier applications, under acknowledgement due, but it was 
returned by the Postal authorities with the endorsement ‘left’ 
The copy of the same letter was sent on December 9. 1974 
by registe'.ed post to the permanent town address of the claim- 
ant in Rayadurg, Srinagar District. That letter was mistakenly 
received by another R. C. Ktila who later icturncd it to the 
Management as per its request. II is also contended by the 
Management that the Petitioner did not funiish any leave 
address or intimated the Management about the change of 
his addres.s though there was a duty ctist upon him as per 
Clause 11 of the Certified Standing Orders. It is, however, 
admitted that on 28th December, 1974 the claimant approach- 
ed wilth a medical fitness certificate and another medical cer- 
tificate upto 27th December, 1974, The contention is that 
since the lien of the claimant on his employment stood auto- 
matically terminaled he could not be taken on duty. The 
representation made by the claimant on Ihe said date and a 
later communication sent by him dated 1T1-I975 had to be 
rejected on a careful scrutiny of the material, In short it 
is alleged that what wa.s intimated to the claimant under the 
various orders is only the automatic lo.s.s of his lien on the 
post and not that his services were terminated by a positive 
act of the Management. It is, further alleged that the claimant 
wa,s an Army Reservist but instead of being disciplined his 
performance is far from being a disciplined one. It is also 
alleged that the Respondent was running a shop attached to 
his father-in-law’s residence at Lalaguda after he abandoned 
his service. It is reiterated that .since no order of termination 
was passed by the Management there is no industrial dispute 
and that the Tribunal is not competent to adjudicate upon 
this reference, 

4. After the Respondent filed Ifis counter the workman was 
not present on two hearings, therefore, a notice was again 
directed to him which was also served. Inspire of service 
notice for the hearing on 21-1-1976 the workman was absent, 
^e case was adjoiuncd finally to 17-2-1976 for enquiry. 
The claimant-workman sent a petition through post stating 
that he is not in a position to move and that the Tribunal 
should decide for itself and intimate the result to him. The 
Management, however, sought time to lead evidence. Thu.o 
M.W. 1 the Industrial Relation', Officer, examined himself 
in support of the averments of the counter. 

5. The evidence of M.W. 1 and records are perused. The 
evidence of M.W. 1 is that no leave application whatsoever 
was received from the claimant ou 6th October, 1974, and 
the first communication received is a medical certificate on 
13lh December, 1974. TTie workman filed a certificate of 
posting dated 6-10-1974 under which a cover is said to have 
been addressed to the Administrtitive Officer. It is also not 
the case of the workman that on 6-10-1974 he enclosed any 
medical certificate to the leave application. The evidence of 
M.W. 1 is that a panel of 39 doctors are spread over the 
twin cities who could be approached by the sick workman. 
If really the workman was suffering from any disease such 
as eosinophillia, nothing prevented him from approaching the 
Company's Doctor. The claimant has also not gone into 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 17^ 1976/CHAlTRA 28, 1898 


1517 


Sec. 3(ii)] 


the witness box to swear in support of the allegation that he 
sent an application on 6th October, 1974. On the other htmd 
the positive evidence of M.W. 1 is that no leave application 
was received. Even before the frrst medical certificate was 
sent by the claimant the office of the Respondent-Manage- 
ment after waiting for 14 days is said to have sent a letter 
on 26th October, 1974 drawing the attention of the claimant 
to Clause 34 of the Standing Orders and calling upon him to 
join duty immediately. As the claimant workman did not 
join duty even thereafter, on 15th November. 1974 he was 
notified through a letter that he lost his lien over the job 
It is true that admittedly that letter could not be served on 
the workman though the Respondent-Managernent even tried 
to effect its service by sending it through registered post on 
bis home town addres.! as furnished by the workman-claimant. 
Non-service of this letter as is borne out by the records and as 
iilso admitted by the Management cannot be held to be a 
factor against the Management. It is equally necessary to 
note that it is not the case of the workman that the Manage- 
ment bore any grudge against him or that the action of the 
Management, if any, was by way of victimisation. There is 
no reason to believe that the Management has suppressed 
the alleged leave application sent by him initially, 'rhere is 
nothing on record to show that the workman has by another 
leave application sought cxiciision of leave. On the other 
hand, the Management has well preserved the communications 
sent by the claimant. The medical certificate sent by him 
is pari of the record. While a leave of 49 days is recom- 
mended the date from 5-10-1974 to 22-11-1974 is altered to 
5-10-1974 to 18-12-1974. That certificate is issued by an 
,'\.sst. Civil Surgeon, though counter-signed by the Civil Sur- 
geon of the Osmania General Hospital. It is rightly con- 
tended by the Management that the alteration of the dales do 
not correspond to the number of days in the said certificate 
and if petitioner could move from Secunderabad to Dabir- 
pura in Hyderabad he could have as well approached the 
Company’s Doctor if in fact he was suffering from any dise.ise. 
The contention of the Management that it is a .spurions medi- 
cal certificate is not devoid of truth. 

(). It is now to Ire seen whether the Standing Orders of the 
Company provide for the automatic termination of the lien, 
C laiise 34 of the Standing Orders reads as follow.s : — 

■‘If a workman remains unauthorisedly absent for 14 
days or moie. such a workman will be deemed to 
have abandoned the employment voluntarily, NFC 
Management may review such cases at its discretion 
depending upon the circumstances and reserve the 
light whether or not to re-employ him and if re- 
ernployed, on such service condition as it may con- 
n.Jer fit.” 

11 Can be recalled that in view of the un-aulborbcd absence 
of the woiknmn from 5th October, 1974 the Management 
issued a Memo on 261 h October, 1974 itself drawing the atten- 
tion ol the workmen to thi* Clause. When the workman 
persisted in his .absence till 15th Noveipbcr, 1974 he was noti- 
fied about the automatic loss of lien. All these letters cma- 
n.ated from the Management even befote the fust medical 
certificate was received by it from the claimant. In the 
absence of any oblique motive attributed to the Management, 
it can only be said that there was no leave application before 
it whatsoever and therefore, the claimant was informed of 
the automatic loss of his lien even bv 15th November. 1975. 
As noted above, the letter notifying the loss of lien could 
not hovicvcr be served unon him. But that is not due to 
the fault of the Management. TTie Standing Order refened 
to above lays down in clear terms that the un-authorised ab- 
sence would be deemed to have resulted in the abandonment 
of employment. There is no positive act of passing an order 
of termination by the Management, Even though where the 
Management artfully couches an order of discharge or dis- 
mksal a.s an order of termination simoliciter the Tribunal 
woukl be competent to go beyontl the order and find out iw 
(rue nature But .such a situation does not arise in this case. 
It is held in Buckingham & Ctirn.atic Co. v. Venkatiah (AIR 
1964 Supt'ornc Court, page 1272) : 

“Whether termination of the employees services follows 
automatically either from a contract or from a Stand- 
ing Order by virtue of the cmnlovee’s absence with- 
out leave for the specified period, such termination 
is not the result of any positive act or order on 
the part of the employer.” 


It is similarly hold in N. E, Industrial v, Hanuman 
(1968 Supreme Court page 83) ; 

“Whether a workman’s service tciminates automatically 
under the Standing Orders, Section 33 would not 
apply 

Where a Standing Order provides ihal a workman 
losses lien on his appointment, if he does not join 
duty within certain time iifter his leave expires it 
only means that his service stands automatically 
terminated when the contingency happens.” 

Thus as per the Standing Orders referred to above tiie work- 
man has lost his lien in view of un-authorised absence for more 
than the minimum period of 15 days. In view of the attempt 
of the workman to procure a medical certificate which on 
account of its suspicious nature throws doubt about his being 
really Ul, the Management is not bound to re-consider his re- 
employment. That apart, it is not only the averment of 
the counter but also the evidence of M.W. J lhat during 
the course of conciliation the workman expressed no :mxiety 
at all about the loss of his job, as he is running a snvall shop. 
The attitude of the workman displayed picsently by sending 
a letter that the case may be decided on meiils and his 
contiiiuoirs absence before (he Tribunal without caring to 
cHtablish his allegations would also indicate that the workman 
is not keen in prosecuting his case. Thus tbcrc i.s no reason 
to call upon the Management either to reinstate or to re- 
employ him on any condition. The dismissal of his represen- 
tation hy the Manngemcnt in the above circumstances appears 
to be justified. M.W. 1 would also iivei that the claimant- 
workman inspite of being an Army Reservist was not disci- 
plined and was not regular in his attendance and was a matter 
of anxiety. Considering this circumstance also there does 
not appear to be any ground directing the re-cmpIoymcnt of 
the workman. In short, the workman is not entitled to any 
relief and his claim Ls. iherefcic, icjccted. The reference is 
answered holding that the Management was justified in treat- 
ing the workman as having abandoned the service voluntarily, 
in the light of Clause 34 of the Standing Orders. 

Award passe,! accordingly. 

Dictated to the Stenographer, tianscribed by him and cor- 
rected by me and given under my hand and the seal of thk 
Tribunal, this the 23rd February, 1976. 

T, NARASING RAO. Industrial Tribunal.
